\usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s \equiv s' \mod NS\mathbb {Z}^{n}$$\end{document}s≡s′modNSZn (see Remark 2). So we can consider the entries \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r-S t$$\end{document}r-St \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ (\textrm{mod}\ NS\mathbb {Z}^{n})$$\end{document}(modNSZn) and all of these are different \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ (\textrm{mod}\ dS\mathbb {Z}^{n})$$\end{document}(moddSZn). \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Poincaré Series

In this Section, we define a special class of Fourier–Jacobi forms, called Poincaré series, which reproduce the Fourier coefficients of Jacobi forms under the Petersson scalar product. What is more, they generate the space of cusp forms and in turn the Maass space, as we will show in the next Section.

Definition 5.1

We define the support of the lattice \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L=\mathbb {Z}^n$$\end{document}L=Zn with respect to the bilinear form \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sigma (x,y)=x^{t}Sy$$\end{document}σ(x,y)=xtSy for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x,y \in V$$\end{document}x,y∈V to be\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \text {supp}(L, \sigma ) := \left\{ (D,r) \mid D \in \mathbb {Q}_{\le 0}, r \in L^{*}, D \equiv \frac{1}{2}S[r] \pmod {\mathbb {Z}}\right\} . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}supp(L,σ):=(D,r)∣D∈Q≤0,r∈L∗,D≡12S[r](modZ).Now, if we write \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r \longmapsto S^{-1}r$$\end{document}r⟼S-1r with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r \in L$$\end{document}r∈L, we get\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} qD \equiv \frac{1}{2}qS^{-1}[r] \pmod {q\mathbb {Z}}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}qD≡12qS-1[r](modqZ),which then implies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$D \in \frac{1}{q}\mathbb {Z}$$\end{document}D∈1qZ. Hence, by writing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$D \longmapsto D/q$$\end{document}D⟼D/q, we get that equivalently\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \text {supp}(L, \sigma ) = \left\{ (D/q,S^{-1}r) \mid D \in \mathbb {Z}_{\le 0}, r \in L, D \equiv \frac{1}{2}qS^{-1}[r] \pmod {q}\right\} . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}supp(L,σ)=(D/q,S-1r)∣D∈Z≤0,r∈L,D≡12qS-1[r](modq).Let then\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \widetilde{\text {supp}}(L, \sigma ) := \{(D,r) \in \mathbb {Z}_{\le 0}\times L \mid (D/q, S^{-1}r) \in \text {supp}(L, \sigma )\}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}supp~(L,σ):={(D,r)∈Z≤0×L∣(D/q,S-1r)∈supp(L,σ)}.and in the following this is the set we will use.

Definition 5.2

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(D,r) \in \widetilde{\text {supp}}(L, \sigma )$$\end{document}(D,r)∈supp~(L,σ). We then define the following complex valued function on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {H} \times (L \otimes \mathbb {C})$$\end{document}H×(L⊗C):\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} g_{D,r}(\tau ,z) := e\left( \frac{\frac{1}{2}qS^{-1}[r]-D}{q}\tau + r^{t}z\right) , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}gD,r(τ,z):=e12qS-1[r]-Dqτ+rtz,where q is the level of L (see Definition 2.4).

Definition 5.3

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(D,r) \in \widetilde{\text {supp}}(L, \sigma )$$\end{document}(D,r)∈supp~(L,σ) and set\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} J^{(L, \sigma )}_{\infty }:=\left\{ \left( \begin{pmatrix}1& n\\ 0& 1\end{pmatrix},(0,\mu )\right) \mid n\in \mathbb {Z}, \mu \in L\right\} . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}J∞(L,σ):=1n01,(0,μ)∣n∈Z,μ∈L.The Poincaré series of weight k for the lattice \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(L, \sigma )$$\end{document}(L,σ) is defined by\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} P_{k,D,r}(\tau ,z) := \sum _{\gamma \in J_{\infty }^{(L,\sigma )} \backslash J^{(L,\sigma )}} g_{D,r} \mid _{k, (L,\sigma )} \gamma (\tau ,z). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Pk,D,r(τ,z):=∑γ∈J∞(L,σ)\J(L,σ)gD,r∣k,(L,σ)γ(τ,z).If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k > n/2+2$$\end{document}k>n/2+2, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$P_{k,D,r}$$\end{document}Pk,D,r is absolutely and uniformly convergent on compact subsets of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {H} \times (L \otimes \mathbb {C})$$\end{document}H×(L⊗C) and defines an element of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$S_k(L, \sigma )$$\end{document}Sk(L,σ) (see [17, Theorem 2.3, (i)]). Moreover, from the same Theorem, it only depends on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$S^{-1}r\pmod L $$\end{document}S-1r(modL).

We now have the following very important property, which can be again found in [17, Theorem 2.3, (i)].

Proposition 5.1

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(D,r) \in \widetilde{\text {supp}}(L, \sigma )$$\end{document}(D,r)∈supp~(L,σ). Then for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f \in S_{k}(L,\sigma )$$\end{document}f∈Sk(L,σ) with\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} f(\tau , z) = \sum _{\begin{array}{c} D' \in \mathbb {Z}_{<0}, r' \in L\\ \frac{1}{2}qS^{-1}[r'] \equiv D' \pmod {q} \end{array}}c_{f}\left( D', r'\right) e\left( \frac{\frac{1}{2}qS^{-1}[r']-D'}{q}\tau + (r')^{t} z\right) , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}f(τ,z)=∑D′∈Z<0,r′∈L12qS-1[r′]≡D′(modq)cfD′,r′e12qS-1[r′]-D′qτ+(r′)tz,we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \langle f, P_{k,D,r} \rangle = \lambda _{k,D}c_{f}(D, r), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}⟨f,Pk,D,r⟩=λk,Dcf(D,r),for some constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda _{k,D} \in \mathbb {C}$$\end{document}λk,D∈C depending on k and D.

Dirichlet Series and relation with Fourier coefficients

In this Section, we define the Dirichlet series of interest. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widetilde{\Gamma }_S \le \Gamma \le \Gamma _S$$\end{document}Γ~S≤Γ≤ΓS be a subgroup. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F, G \in S_k(\Gamma )$$\end{document}F,G∈Sk(Γ) with corresponding Fourier–Jacobi coefficients \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{\phi _m\}_{m=1}^{\infty }, \{\psi _m\}_{m=1}^{\infty }$$\end{document}{ϕm}m=1∞,{ψm}m=1∞ respectively (see (3)). We define the Dirichlet series7\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {D}_{F,G}(s) := \sum _{N=1}^{\infty }\langle \phi _N, \psi _N\rangle N^{-s}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}DF,G(s):=∑N=1∞⟨ϕN,ψN⟩N-s,where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\langle \ , \ \rangle $$\end{document}⟨,⟩ denotes the inner product of Definition 3.5. We then have the following Lemma.

Lemma 6.1

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {D}_{F,G}(s)$$\end{document}DF,G(s) converges absolutely for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {Re}(s) > k+1$$\end{document}Re(s)>k+1 and represents a holomorphic function in this domain.

Proof

The proof is similar to [13, Lemma 1]. We will show for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N \ge 1$$\end{document}N≥1 that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \left\langle \phi _N, \psi _N \right\rangle = \mathcal {O}(N^{k}), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ϕN,ψN=O(Nk),with the constant depending only on F, G. Indeed, fix \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\tau , z) \in \mathbb {H} \times \mathbb {C}^{n}$$\end{document}(τ,z)∈H×Cn and write \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau = u+iv, \quad z = x+iy$$\end{document}τ=u+iv,z=x+iy. If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F(q) = \sum _{N=1}^{\infty }\phi _{N}(\tau ,z)q^{N}$$\end{document}F(q)=∑N=1∞ϕN(τ,z)qN, with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$q=e^{2\pi i \tau '}$$\end{document}q=e2πiτ′, we have by Cauchy’s integral formula that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \phi _{N}(\tau ,z) = \oint _{|q|=r}\frac{F(q)}{q^{N+1}}\text {d}q, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ϕN(τ,z)=∮|q|=rF(q)qN+1dq,for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0< r < e^{-\pi S[y]/v}$$\end{document}0<r<e-πS[y]/v. The bounds follow from the fact that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$S_0[\text {Im}Z]>0$$\end{document}S0[ImZ]>0 (here \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Z = (\tau ',z,\tau ) \in \mathcal {H}_S$$\end{document}Z=(τ′,z,τ)∈HS). If now \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau ' = u'+iv'$$\end{document}τ′=u′+iv′, the integral can be written as\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \phi _{N}(\tau ,z) = \int _{0}^{1}F(Z)e^{-2\pi i N \tau '}\text {d}u', \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ϕN(τ,z)=∫01F(Z)e-2πiNτ′du′,for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v' > S[y]/2v$$\end{document}v′>S[y]/2v. But now \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left| F(Z)\right| \left( S_0[\text {Im}Z]/2\right) ^{k/2}$$\end{document}F(Z)S0[ImZ]/2k/2 is bounded on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {H}_S$$\end{document}HS from [11, II, Lemma 3.28], say by a constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C>0$$\end{document}C>0. Therefore, after choosing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v'=S[y]/2v+1/N$$\end{document}v′=S[y]/2v+1/N, we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \left| \phi _{N}(\tau ,z)\right| \le Ce^{2\pi }\int _{0}^{1}\left( S_0[\text {Im}Z]/2\right) ^{-k/2}e^{\pi N S[y]/v}\text {d}u' = Ce^{2\pi }\left( \frac{v}{N}\right) ^{-k/2}e^{ \pi N S[y]v}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ϕN(τ,z)≤Ce2π∫01S0[ImZ]/2-k/2eπNS[y]/vdu′=Ce2πvN-k/2eπNS[y]v.Similarly for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\psi _N$$\end{document}ψN and then the claim follows from the definition of the inner product in Definition 3.5. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

From now on, we let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k>n/2+2$$\end{document}k>n/2+2 be an even integer. Let then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F \in S_k(\Gamma _S)$$\end{document}F∈Sk(ΓS) and write \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi _N$$\end{document}ϕN for its Fourier–Jacobi coefficients. For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(D,r) \in \widetilde{\text {supp}}(L, \sigma )$$\end{document}(D,r)∈supp~(L,σ), let also8\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {P}_{k,D,r}(\tau ', z,\tau ) := \sum _{N \ge 1}V_{N}P_{k,D,r}(\tau ,z)e(N\tau '). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Pk,D,r(τ′,z,τ):=∑N≥1VNPk,D,r(τ,z)e(Nτ′).From [28, Corollary 6.7], we have that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {P}_{k,D,r} \in S_{k}^{*}(\widetilde{\Gamma }_S)$$\end{document}Pk,D,r∈Sk∗(Γ~S) (see Definition 2.6). Here, by abusing notation, we write \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$P_{k,D,r}$$\end{document}Pk,D,r to actually denote the Poincaré series \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\displaystyle {\frac{1}{\lambda _{k,D}}P_{k,D,r}}$$\end{document}1λk,DPk,D,r, with the quantities defined in Proposition 5.1. Then, we have the Dirichlet series\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {D}_{F,\mathcal {P}_{k,D,r}}(s) := \sum _{N=1}^{\infty } \langle \phi _{N}, V_{N}P_{k,D,r}\rangle N^{-s} = \sum _{N=1}^{\infty } \langle V_{N}^{*}\phi _{N}, P_{k,D,r}\rangle N^{-s}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}DF,Pk,D,r(s):=∑N=1∞⟨ϕN,VNPk,D,r⟩N-s=∑N=1∞⟨VN∗ϕN,Pk,D,r⟩N-s.

Remark 4

Even though F and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {P}_{k,D,r}$$\end{document}Pk,D,r are taken invariant with respect to different modular groups, we note here that the proof of Lemma 6.1 is still valid, because \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F \in S_k(\Gamma _{S}) \subset S_k(\widetilde{\Gamma }_S)$$\end{document}F∈Sk(ΓS)⊂Sk(Γ~S).

Remark 5

A corollary of [17, Theorem 2.3 (i)] is that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$S_k(L, \sigma )$$\end{document}Sk(L,σ) is generated by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left\{ P_{k,D,r} \mid (D,r) \in \widetilde{\text {supp}}(L, \sigma ), \ S^{-1}r \pmod L\right\} $$\end{document}Pk,D,r∣(D,r)∈supp~(L,σ),S-1r(modL) (cf. [17, Corollary 2.4]). This observation, together with [28, Corollary 6.7] gives us that the Maass space \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$S_{k}^{*}(\widetilde{\Gamma }_S)$$\end{document}Sk∗(Γ~S) is generated by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left\{ \mathcal {P}_{k,D,r} \mid (D,r) \in \widetilde{\text {supp}}(L, \sigma ), \ S^{-1}r \pmod L\right\} $$\end{document}Pk,D,r∣(D,r)∈supp~(L,σ),S-1r(modL). It is therefore enough to consider G to be a Poincaré series in (7).

We will now relate the Nth term of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {D}_{F,\mathcal {P}_{k,D,r}}(s)$$\end{document}DF,Pk,D,r(s) with the Fourier coefficients of F.

Proposition 6.1

With the notation as above and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N \ge 1$$\end{document}N≥1 we have:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \langle V_N^* \phi _N, P_{k,D,r} \rangle = \sum _{d \mid N} d^{k-(n+1)} \sum _{\begin{array}{c} s \mod dS\mathbb {Z}^{n}\\ D \equiv \frac{1}{2}qS^{-1}[s] \mod qd \end{array}}A\left( \frac{N}{d}\left( \frac{\frac{1}{2}qS^{-1}[s]-D}{qd}, S^{-1}s, d\right) \right) . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}⟨VN∗ϕN,Pk,D,r⟩=∑d∣Ndk-(n+1)∑smoddSZnD≡12qS-1[s]modqdANd12qS-1[s]-Dqd,S-1s,d.

Proof

F admits a Fourier expansion of the form (see equation (2))\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} F(Z) = \sum _{\tilde{r} \in L_0^{*}}A(\tilde{r})e({\widetilde{r}}^{t}S_0Z) = \sum _{N=1}^{\infty } \phi _{N}(\tau , z)e(N\tau '), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}F(Z)=∑r~∈L0∗A(r~)e(r~tS0Z)=∑N=1∞ϕN(τ,z)e(Nτ′),where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Z = (\tau ', z, \tau ) \in \mathcal {H}_S$$\end{document}Z=(τ′,z,τ)∈HS and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{r} = (m, r, N)$$\end{document}r~=(m,r,N) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r \in L^{*}$$\end{document}r∈L∗. We can then write\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \phi _{N}(\tau , z) = \sum _{m \in \mathbb {Z}, r \in L^{*}}A(m,r,N)e(m\tau - r^{t}Sz) = \sum _{m \in \mathbb {Z}, r \in \mathbb {Z}^{n}}A(m, S^{-1}r, N)e(m\tau -r^{t} z). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ϕN(τ,z)=∑m∈Z,r∈L∗A(m,r,N)e(mτ-rtSz)=∑m∈Z,r∈ZnA(m,S-1r,N)e(mτ-rtz).But now \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{pmatrix}-1_2 & & \\ & 1_n& \\ & & -1_2\end{pmatrix} \in \Gamma _{S}$$\end{document}-121n-12∈ΓS and therefore if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Z=(\tau ',z,\tau ) \in \mathcal {H}_S$$\end{document}Z=(τ′,z,τ)∈HS, we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} F\left( (\tau ',-z,\tau )\right) = (-1)^kF((\tau ',z,\tau )) = F((\tau ',z,\tau )), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}F(τ′,-z,τ)=(-1)kF((τ′,z,τ))=F((τ′,z,τ)),which then implies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A(m, r, N) = A(m, -r, N)$$\end{document}A(m,r,N)=A(m,-r,N) for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$m, N \in \mathbb {Z}, \ r \in L^{*}$$\end{document}m,N∈Z,r∈L∗. Relation to the class number

In this Section, our goal is to bring \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {D}_{F, \mathcal {P}_{k,D,r}}(s)$$\end{document}DF,Pk,D,r(s) into a form similar to the one in [13, page 553]. We first need some definitions of the adelized groups and of genus and classes of lattices.

Let V denote any finite dimensional vector space over \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {Q}$$\end{document}Q of dimension \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n \ge 1$$\end{document}n≥1. For each prime p (including infinity), we define \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$V_p := V \otimes _{\mathbb {Q}} \mathbb {Q}_p$$\end{document}Vp:=V⊗QQp. For a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {Z}$$\end{document}Z-lattice L in V, we denote by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L_p:= L \otimes _{\mathbb {Z}}\mathbb {Z}_p$$\end{document}Lp:=L⊗ZZp. This coincides with the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {Z}_p$$\end{document}Zp-linear span of L in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$V_p$$\end{document}Vp.

Definition 7.1

We define the adelizations V and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {GL}(V)$$\end{document}GL(V), as follows:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} V_{\mathbb {A}} = \left\{ v \in \prod _{p\le \infty }V_p \mid v_p \in L_p \text { for almost all } p < \infty \right\} , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}VA=v∈∏p≤∞Vp∣vp∈Lpforalmostallp<∞,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \text {GL}(V)_{\mathbb {A}} = \left\{ \alpha \in \prod _{p\le \infty }\text {GL}(V_p) \mid \alpha _pL_p = L_p \text { for almost all } p < \infty \right\} . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}GL(V)A=α∈∏p≤∞GL(Vp)∣αpLp=Lpforalmostallp<∞.

Here L is an arbitrary \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {Z}$$\end{document}Z-lattice, and the above definitions do not depend on the choice of it, by virtue of Proposition 7.1.

Remark 6

When we identify V with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {Q}^{n}$$\end{document}Qn, we identify \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {GL}(V)$$\end{document}GL(V) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {GL}_{n}(\mathbb {Q})$$\end{document}GLn(Q). Then, we identify \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {GL}_{n}(\mathbb {Q})_{\mathbb {A}}$$\end{document}GLn(Q)A with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {GL}_{n}(\mathbb {Q}_{\mathbb {A}})$$\end{document}GLn(QA), where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {Q}_{\mathbb {A}}$$\end{document}QA is the usual ring of adeles.

Remark 7

Given \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x \in \text {GL}(V)_{\mathbb {A}}$$\end{document}x∈GL(V)A, we have that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x_pL_{p} = L_p$$\end{document}xpLp=Lp for almost all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p < \infty $$\end{document}p<∞. By Proposition 7.1, there is a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {Z}$$\end{document}Z-lattice M such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M_p = x_pL_{p}$$\end{document}Mp=xpLp for all p. We denote this lattice by xL. Hence, xL is the lattice which has the property \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(xL)_p = x_pL_p$$\end{document}(xL)p=xpLp for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p < \infty $$\end{document}p<∞.

Definition 7.2

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G \subset \hbox {GL}_{n}(\mathbb {Q})$$\end{document}G⊂GLn(Q) any algebraic linear group. For any field K containing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {Q}$$\end{document}Q, we denote by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_{K}$$\end{document}GK the group of K-rational points in G. For any prime number p, we abbreviate by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_p$$\end{document}Gp the group \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_{\mathbb {Q}_p}$$\end{document}GQp. Let also \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_{\infty }$$\end{document}G∞ denote \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_{\mathbb {R}}$$\end{document}GR. We then define\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} G_{\mathbb {A}} := \left\{ x \in \prod _{p\le \infty } G_p \mid x_pL_p = L_p \text { for almost all } p\right\} . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}GA:=x∈∏p≤∞Gp∣xpLp=Lpforalmostallp.Moreover, we use the notation \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_{\mathbb {A},f}$$\end{document}GA,f to denote the finite part of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_{\mathbb {A}}$$\end{document}GA.

Definition 7.3

Let L denote a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {Z}$$\end{document}Z-lattice in V and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G \subset \hbox {GL}_n(\mathbb {Q})$$\end{document}G⊂GLn(Q) any algebraic group. Then, for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x \in G$$\end{document}x∈G, xL is also a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {Z}$$\end{document}Z-lattice in V. We define the class of L to be \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{xL \mid x \in G\}$$\end{document}{xL∣x∈G}. Similarly, for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x \in G_{\mathbb {A}}$$\end{document}x∈GA, xL is again a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {Z}$$\end{document}Z-lattice in V. The set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{xL \mid x \in G_{\mathbb {A}}\}$$\end{document}{xL∣x∈GA} is called the genus of L.

Now, the genus of L can be decomposed into disjoint union of classes. If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C = \{x \in G_{\mathbb {A}} \mid xL=L\}$$\end{document}C={x∈GA∣xL=L}, the map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$xC \longmapsto xL$$\end{document}xC⟼xL gives a bijection between \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_{\mathbb {A}}/C$$\end{document}GA/C and the genus of L, so gives a bijection between \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G\backslash G_{\mathbb {A}}/C$$\end{document}G\GA/C and the set of classes contained in the genus of L. In general, if U is an open subgroup of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_{\mathbb {A}}$$\end{document}GA, we call the number \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\# (G\backslash G_{\mathbb {A}}/U)$$\end{document}#(G\GA/U) the class number of G with respect to U.

Now, with all the main definitions out of the way, we want to deal with the vectors appearing in Proposition 6.1. For a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {Z}$$\end{document}Z-lattice \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda $$\end{document}Λ in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$V_0$$\end{document}V0, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {b}$$\end{document}b a fractional ideal of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {Q}$$\end{document}Q and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$q \in \mathbb {Q}^{\times }$$\end{document}q∈Q×, we define\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} := \left\{ x \in V_0 \mid \phi _0[x] = q \text { and } \phi _0(x, \Lambda ) = \mathfrak {b}\right\} . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Λ[q,b]:=x∈V0∣ϕ0[x]=qandϕ0(x,Λ)=b.We then have the following Lemma.

Lemma 7.1

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$D \in \mathbb {Z}_{\le 0}$$\end{document}D∈Z≤0 and define the set\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Xi _{d}:= \left\{ \xi = \left( \frac{\frac{1}{2}qS^{-1}[s]-D}{qd}, S^{-1}s, d\right) ^{t} \mid s \mod dS\mathbb {Z}^{n}, \ D \equiv \frac{1}{2}qS^{-1}[s] \mod qd\right\} . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Ξd:=ξ=12qS-1[s]-Dqd,S-1s,dt∣smoddSZn,D≡12qS-1[s]modqd.We then have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Xi _{d} \subset L_0\left[ -\frac{D}{q}, \frac{1}{2}\mathbb {Z}\right] \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Ξd⊂L0-Dq,12Zfor all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d \ge 1$$\end{document}d≥1 coprime to D.

Proof

Firstly, for any vector \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi \in \Xi _{d}$$\end{document}ξ∈Ξd, we can directly compute \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi _0[\xi ] = -D/q$$\end{document}ϕ0[ξ]=-D/q. It now remains to show that the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {Z}$$\end{document}Z-ideal\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \left\{ \xi ^{t}S_0x \mid x \in \mathbb {Z}^{n+2}\right\} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ξtS0x∣x∈Zn+2equals \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {Z}$$\end{document}Z (because the bilinear form is \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi _0 = S_0/2$$\end{document}ϕ0=S0/2). But for any basis vector \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$e_i$$\end{document}ei of the lattice \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {Z}^{n+2}$$\end{document}Zn+2, we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \xi ^{t}S_0e_i = {\left\{ \begin{array}{ll} d & \text {if }i=1\\ -s_{i-1} & \text {if } 2 \le i \le n+1\\ \frac{\frac{1}{2}qS^{-1}[s]-D}{qd} & \text {if } i=n+2 \end{array}\right. }, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ξtS0ei=difi=1-si-1if2≤i≤n+112qS-1[s]-Dqdifi=n+2,so the above ideal is contained in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {Z}$$\end{document}Z by the conditions that define the set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Xi _{d}$$\end{document}Ξd. Now, if it were equal to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k\mathbb {Z}$$\end{document}kZ for some \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k \ge 1$$\end{document}k≥1, then we would have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} k \mid d, \ k\mid s, \text { and } k \mid \frac{\frac{1}{2}qS^{-1}[s]-D}{qd}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}k∣d,k∣s,andk∣12qS-1[s]-Dqd.This would then imply \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k \mid D$$\end{document}k∣D, and so \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k=1$$\end{document}k=1, as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d, \ D$$\end{document}d,D are assumed to be coprime. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

We now have the following very important Lemma.

Lemma 7.2

The class number of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_{\mathbb {Q}}^{*}$$\end{document}GQ∗, defined as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\# (G^{*}_{\mathbb {Q}}\backslash G^{*}_{\mathbb {A}}/C)$$\end{document}#(GQ∗\GA∗/C), where11\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} C = \{x \in G^{*}_{\mathbb {A}} \mid xL_0 = L_0\}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}C={x∈GA∗∣xL0=L0},is 1.

Proof

This is shown in [26, Remark 2.4, (5)], which is an improvement of [24, Theorem 9.26], as the technical assumptions are weakened. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Assume now it exists \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi \in L_0\left[ -\frac{D}{q}, \frac{1}{2}\mathbb {Z}\right] $$\end{document}ξ∈L0-Dq,12Z and consider the algebraic subgroup of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_{\mathbb {Q}}^{*}$$\end{document}GQ∗\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} H(\xi )_{\mathbb {Q}} = \left\{ g \in G_{\mathbb {Q}}^{*} \mid g\xi = \xi \right\} . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}H(ξ)Q=g∈GQ∗∣gξ=ξ.We note that if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W := \{x \in V_0 \mid \phi _0(x, \xi )=0\}$$\end{document}W:={x∈V0∣ϕ0(x,ξ)=0} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\psi := \phi _0 \mid _{W}$$\end{document}ψ:=ϕ0∣W, then\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} H(\xi )_{\mathbb {Q}} = \hbox {SO}^{\psi }(W)=\{\alpha \in \text {GL}(W) \mid \psi [\alpha w] = \psi [w], \ \forall w \in W\}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}H(ξ)Q=SOψ(W)={α∈GL(W)∣ψ[αw]=ψ[w],∀w∈W}.We now have the following Proposition, which is a special case of [26, Theorem 2.2].

Proposition 7.2

There is a bijection\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} L_0\left[ -\frac{D}{q}, \frac{1}{2}\mathbb {Z}\right] /\Gamma (L_0) \longleftrightarrow H(\xi )_{\mathbb {Q}}\backslash H(\xi )_{\mathbb {A}}/(H(\xi )_{\mathbb {A}}\cap C), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}L0-Dq,12Z/Γ(L0)⟷H(ξ)Q\H(ξ)A/(H(ξ)A∩C),which is given as follows:

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k \in L_0\left[ -\frac{D}{q}, \frac{1}{2}\mathbb {Z}\right] $$\end{document}k∈L0-Dq,12Z, then there is some \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha \in G^{*}_{\mathbb {Q}}$$\end{document}α∈GQ∗ such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k = \alpha \xi $$\end{document}k=αξ (Witt’s theorem, [24, Lemma 1.5 (ii)]). We then assign the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$H(\xi )_{\mathbb {Q}}$$\end{document}H(ξ)Q-class of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha ^{-1}L_0$$\end{document}α-1L0 to k. In particular, this then gives\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \# \left( L_0\left[ -\frac{D}{q}, \frac{1}{2}\mathbb {Z}\right] /\Gamma (L_0)\right) = \# (H(\xi )_{\mathbb {Q}}\backslash H(\xi )_{\mathbb {A}}/(H(\xi )_{\mathbb {A}}\cap C)) . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}#L0-Dq,12Z/Γ(L0)=#(H(ξ)Q\H(ξ)A/(H(ξ)A∩C)).

Proof

First of all, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Gamma (L_0)$$\end{document}Γ(L0) acts on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L_0\left[ -\frac{D}{q}, \frac{1}{2}\mathbb {Z}\right] $$\end{document}L0-Dq,12Z. This can be seen because if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\gamma \in \Gamma (L_0), x \in L_0\left[ -\frac{D}{q}, \frac{1}{2}\mathbb {Z}\right] $$\end{document}γ∈Γ(L0),x∈L0-Dq,12Z, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi _0[\gamma x] = \phi _0[x] = -D/q$$\end{document}ϕ0[γx]=ϕ0[x]=-D/q and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi _0(\gamma x, L_0) = \phi _0(\gamma x, \gamma L_0) = \phi _0(x, L_0) = \frac{1}{2}\mathbb {Z}$$\end{document}ϕ0(γx,L0)=ϕ0(γx,γL0)=ϕ0(x,L0)=12Z.

The remaining assertions follow from [26, Theorem 2.2, (iv)], because \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_\mathbb {Q}^{*}$$\end{document}GQ∗ has class number one (Lemma 7.2) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_\mathbb {Q}^{*} \cap C = \Gamma (L_0)$$\end{document}GQ∗∩C=Γ(L0). \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Let us now write12\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} L_0\left[ -\frac{D}{q}, \frac{1}{2}\mathbb {Z}\right] = \bigsqcup _{i=1}^{h}\Gamma (L_0)\xi _i. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}L0-Dq,12Z=⨆i=1hΓ(L0)ξi.with some \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi _i \in L_0\left[ -\frac{D}{q}, \frac{1}{2}\mathbb {Z}\right] $$\end{document}ξi∈L0-Dq,12Z. This in particular implies that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi _i \in L_0^{*}$$\end{document}ξi∈L0∗ for all i.

We now assert we can take\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \xi _i \in \mathcal {P}_{S} = \{y'=(y_1,y,y_2)\in \mathbb {R}^{n+2} \mid y_1>0, \ \phi _0[y']>0\} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ξi∈PS={y′=(y1,y,y2)∈Rn+2∣y1>0,ϕ0[y′]>0}of Section 2 for all i. The second condition is clear, as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$S_0[\xi _i]=-2D/q>0$$\end{document}S0[ξi]=-2D/q>0 because we take \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$D<0$$\end{document}D<0. For the first one, we can always multiply with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {diag}(-1, 1_n, -1) \in \Gamma (L_0)$$\end{document}diag(-1,1n,-1)∈Γ(L0) and the assertion follows.

Now, if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi \in \Xi _d$$\end{document}ξ∈Ξd of Lemma 7.1, we can write \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi = \gamma \xi _j$$\end{document}ξ=γξj for some \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1 \le j \le h$$\end{document}1≤j≤h and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\gamma \in \Gamma (L_0)$$\end{document}γ∈Γ(L0). But, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi \in \mathcal {P}_S$$\end{document}ξ∈PS as well, so we must have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\gamma \in \Gamma (L_0) \cap G^{*,0}_{\mathbb {R}} = \Gamma ^{+}(L_0)$$\end{document}γ∈Γ(L0)∩GR∗,0=Γ+(L0). Indeed, if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\gamma \in G_{\mathbb {R}}^{*}\backslash G_{\mathbb {R}}^{*,0}$$\end{document}γ∈GR∗\GR∗,0, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widetilde{\gamma }:=\text {diag}(1, \gamma , 1) \in G_{\mathbb {R}}\backslash G_{\mathbb {R}}^{0}$$\end{document}γ~:=diag(1,γ,1)∈GR\GR0. But then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widetilde{\gamma }\langle i\xi _j\rangle = i\xi $$\end{document}γ~⟨iξj⟩=iξ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i\xi ,\ i\xi _j \in \mathcal {H}_S$$\end{document}iξ,iξj∈HS. This is a contradiction, because \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widetilde{\gamma }$$\end{document}γ~ sends \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {H}_S$$\end{document}HS to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$-\mathcal {H}_S := \{z=x-iy \in V_0 \otimes _{\mathbb {R}} \mathbb {C} \mid y \in \mathcal {P}_S\}$$\end{document}-HS:={z=x-iy∈V0⊗RC∣y∈PS} (see [21, p. 18]).

Therefore, from [21, p. 26], we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A(\xi ) = A(\gamma \xi _i) = A(\xi _i)$$\end{document}A(ξ)=A(γξi)=A(ξi). Define now\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} & n(\xi _i;d) := \#\left\{ s \in \mathbb {Z}^{n}/dS\mathbb {Z}^{n} \mid D \equiv \frac{1}{2}qS^{-1}[s] \pmod {qd}, \,\, \left( \frac{\frac{1}{2}qS^{-1}[s]-D}{qd}, S^{-1}s, d\right) ^{t} = \gamma \xi _i, \right. \\ & \quad \left. \gamma \in \Gamma ^{+}(L_0)\right\} , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}n(ξi;d):=#s∈Zn/dSZn∣D≡12qS-1[s](modqd),12qS-1[s]-Dqd,S-1s,dt=γξi,γ∈Γ+(L0),From the above considerations, Lemma 7.1 and Proposition 6.1, we may write, for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(N,D)=1$$\end{document}(N,D)=1\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \langle V_N^* \phi _N, P_{k,D,r} \rangle = \sum _{i=1}^h \sum _{0< d|N} d^{k-(n+1)}n(\xi _i; d) A\left( \frac{N}{d} \xi _i\right) . $$\end{document}⟨VN∗ϕN,Pk,D,r⟩=∑i=1h∑0<d|Ndk-(n+1)n(ξi;d)ANdξi.In particular, we arrive at the following Proposition.

Proposition 7.3

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(D,r) \in \widetilde{\text {supp}}(L, \sigma )$$\end{document}(D,r)∈supp~(L,σ). Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {P}$$\end{document}P be a finite set of primes, which includes the prime factors of D. Let\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {D}_{F,\mathcal {P}_{k,D,r},\mathcal {P}}(s) := \sum _{\begin{array}{c} N=1\\ (N,p)=1\forall p \in \mathcal {P} \end{array}}^{\infty } \langle V_N^* \phi _N, P_{k,D,r}\rangle N^{-s}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}DF,Pk,D,r,P(s):=∑N=1(N,p)=1∀p∈P∞⟨VN∗ϕN,Pk,D,r⟩N-s,which converges absolutely for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {Re}(s)>k+1$$\end{document}Re(s)>k+1 by comparison with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$D_{F,\mathcal {P}_{k,D,r}}(s)$$\end{document}DF,Pk,D,r(s) (see Lemma 6.1). Let also\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \zeta _{\xi _i, \mathcal {P}}(s):= \sum _{\begin{array}{c} N=1\\ (N,p)=1 \forall p\in \mathcal {P} \end{array}}^{\infty } n(\xi _i; N)N^{-s}. $$\end{document}ζξi,P(s):=∑N=1(N,p)=1∀p∈P∞n(ξi;N)N-s.We then have that13\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {D}_{F,\mathcal {P}_{k,D,r},\mathcal {P}}(s) = \sum _{i=1}^h \zeta _{\xi _i,\mathcal {P}}(s-k+n+1) D_{F,\xi _i, \mathcal {P}}(s), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}DF,Pk,D,r,P(s)=∑i=1hζξi,P(s-k+n+1)DF,ξi,P(s),where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$D_{F,\xi _i,\mathcal {P}}(s) := \sum _{\begin{array}{c} N=1\\ (N,p)=1 \forall p\in \mathcal {P} \end{array}}^{\infty } A(N \xi _i) N^{-s}$$\end{document}DF,ξi,P(s):=∑N=1(N,p)=1∀p∈P∞A(Nξi)N-s.

Relation to Sugano’s Theorem

In order to now obtain an Euler product, we will make use of the main theorem of Sugano in his paper [27]. We first need some setup.

For each prime number \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p < \infty $$\end{document}p<∞, we define \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K_{p} := G_{p} \cap \text {SL}_{n+4}(\mathbb {Z}_{p}) = G(\mathbb {Z}_p)$$\end{document}Kp:=Gp∩SLn+4(Zp)=G(Zp) and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K_{f} := \prod _{p < \infty } K_{p}$$\end{document}Kf:=∏p<∞Kp.

We remind ourselves here that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_{\infty }^{0}$$\end{document}G∞0 acts transitively on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {H}_{S}$$\end{document}HS (cf. Section 2). Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {Z}_0$$\end{document}Z0 denote any point of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {H}_{S}$$\end{document}HS with real part 0 and denote by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K_{\infty }$$\end{document}K∞ its stabiliser in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_{\infty }^{0}$$\end{document}G∞0. Then, we have that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_{\infty }^{0}/K_{\infty } \cong \mathcal {H}_{S}$$\end{document}G∞0/K∞≅HS.

Definition 8.1
